Sample script section

IllustrativeWritten for the report to show several suggestions working together. Topic: which of three cheap tools clears which sink clog. Voice: The Vintage Pipefitter. No tenure number and no name intro. Prices are typical late-2026 ranges, to be checked against the fact bank before use.

Title: Plunger, Zip Strip or Hand Auger? Which One Your Sink Clog Needs

[1]Slow bathroom sink? Reach for the zip strip first. Kitchen sink stopped dead? That's a job for the hand auger. And the plunger only earns its keep once you've sealed the overflow.

[2]All three together cost about $35 to $50 at a hardware store. A drain call usually starts around $150 and can run past $300.

[3]By the end you'll know which tool fits your clog, and the one sign that means you put all three down and pick up the phone.

[4]First up, the cup plunger: the plain rubber dome, no folding flap, about $6 to $10. Verdict: fine for a soft clog in one sink, worthless unless a wet rag is pressed over that little slot near the rim of the basin.

[5]Second, the zip strip: a flat plastic strip about two feet long, barbed down both edges, about $3. Verdict: the best first move in any bathroom sink or tub, because those clogs are hair, and hair collects just under the stopper. Lift the stopper out, feed the strip down, pull up slow.

[6]Third, the hand auger: a quarter-inch steel cable coiled in a drum with a crank, about $25 to $40. Verdict: the kitchen tool. Grease sets past the U-bend, inside the wall, where plastic can't reach. Unscrew the U-bend over a bucket and feed the cable into the pipe in the wall.

[7]Uncle Ray kept all three in one bucket and called it the cheapest service van in town.

[8]The one to leave alone: if the tub gurgles when the sink drains, or two fixtures back up at once, the trouble is in the main line. That's a call to a licensed plumber.

[9]So: bathroom, the zip strip. Kitchen, the auger. Either way, plunge only with the overflow sealed. Two drains at once, the phone.

[10]My e-book, [title from persona profile], is free.
Tap the first link in the description and tell me where to send it.

(About 330 words including the title; one long mid-video ask removed, per your decision.)

Which line shows which suggestion

LineSuggestionWhat it shows
Title + [4]-[6](a)The title asks "which one", and every contender gets a verdict, so the body pays off the title literally. No "every clog" or "forever" to walk back; [8] covers the limit the title never claimed.
[1](g)The answer in the first three sentences, by name, before any explanation. No name or channel intro.
[2](b)Fix cost against call-out cost, both as "about" ranges, inside the first 20 seconds.
[3](g)A concrete tease (the one sign to stop and phone) instead of "stay till the end". Paid off in [8].
[4], [5], [6](h)The verdict skeleton: each contender named, priced and given a one-line verdict (what it's for, what it isn't).
[4], [5], [6](b)A price on each tool, so the viewer can add up the kit.
[4]-[6], [8](c)Every beat names a filmable object (a rubber dome, a wet rag, the overflow slot, a barbed strip, a stopper, a cable drum, a U-bend over a bucket, a gurgling tub). No "look at this", no "on the left".
[7](i) (optional)One short Uncle Ray line that adds character, not proof. It sits after the answer, so it doesn't slow the opening. Delete it if (i) is rejected.
[8](a), safetyThe honest limit stated once, compactly, with the call-a-pro moment in one place.
[9](d), (h)A one-breath recap of every step, as job-by-job assignments. Nothing earlier is repeated.
[10](f)The decided Vintage CTA: two lines, a free e-book, at the end only.

Related slips in the same scripts

  • Numbers said twice, 3 times in the toilet-ring video (hOz49worQJg): "Number four. Number four." at 7:00, "Number seven. Number seven." at 12:23, "Number nine. Number nine." at 15:40. A hand count finds about 9 doubled calls in that video in all, and 19 across 4 TheCheapFix recent videos.
  • Wrong channel name: the same video says "the Vintage Plumber" instead of the Vintage Pipefitter.
  • A thumbnail referenced in the script: Mark's grab-bar video (XGWQ3l_zEWA) says Now, the thumbnail on this video says 300 lb. It isn't a production note, but the script talks about the packaging rather than the subject. Vintage bSrEWmK0g5E does the same: That picture you clicked on, the pipe bursting wide open.

The fix is a scanner check that fails any script containing these labels, so they can't reach the voiceover again. The scanner doesn't look for them today. See the next section.

Scanner checks

You said the scanner checks matter a great deal. The core checks are built (20 Oct), tested against all 125 real transcripts and covered by automatic tests. The plumbing ones go in with the plumbing script rules.

Built: the core checks. They block a script until it is fixed, except the packaging mention, which is flagged for review. Persona facts come from each channel's profile (name, book, years in the trade), so nothing is written twice. Hank and Dale keep their experience numberless, so any years figure in their scripts fails.

Where the detail lives

In the script-system repository, folder studies/plumbing/:

  • comparison/README.md: the summary, your decisions and the production-note count as text.
  • comparison/stats.md: the measured numbers for all 80 videos.
  • comparison/01-own-channels.md: your three channels, hits against recent, with every quote and timestamp; 15 suggestions (S1 to S15).
  • comparison/02-competitors.md: each competitor, what carries over to a faceless channel, and a close look at TheCheapFix.
  • comparison/03-own-vs-competitors.md: side by side on 12 dimensions, ranked top 10.
  • comparison/04-hooks.md: 19 hook structures with verbatim openings, all 80 openings classified, the first 60 seconds beat by beat, teases, the hook-to-title handoff and a ranked shortlist.
  • comparison/05-emotions.md: 18 emotional triggers with verbatim lines and comment evidence, six hits mapped minute by minute, the pain-point map, the language bank and what backfired.
  • comparison/06-titles-and-promises.md: 25 title formulas with views against normal, title parts that recur in hits, a promise-payoff audit of all 30 of your videos, and 14 suggested title-to-script rules.
  • comparison/07-body-craft.md: after the hook: 12 item shapes, ordering, transitions, specifics, proof, money framing, safety wording, writing for compiled footage, padding, CTAs and endings, and 39 stock phrases counted.
  • comparison/08-suggestions-with-examples.md: every medium and weaker suggestion with real examples, before-and-after rewrites, draft rule wording (not applied) and a sample script section.
  • transcripts-combined/: every transcript, one file per channel, ready to paste into a chat.
